I'm just so afraid people will look at me and notice that I'm not holding things together the way I used to. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too silly or weak... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you feel like you can't stop or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yes. I guess so. It's a little hard to admit, because I really don't want you to think I just have zero willpower or anything like that. But... yeah. \n\nIt's almost like, once I take that very first bite, it just feels like the decision has already been made for me. I'll actually be standing there, telling myself in my head, 'George, stop now, you don't need any more,' but... it's like my hands just keep right on fixing another plate or opening another container anyway. I really do try to fight it, I promise, it just gets away from me. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it the right way... is that what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. I really try to be mindful about my meals, I promise I do, but... when it gets like that in the evenings, I guess I do speed up quite a bit. \n\nActually, if I'm being completely honest—and I really hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—I eat so fast during those times that I barely even taste the food. It's a bit sad, really, because I used to take so much pride in my cooking. But now... sometimes I'm just standing right there at the kitchen counter, swallowing these big bites before I’ve even finished chewing the last one. \n\nI know that probably sounds awful, and I really wish I could just slow down and enjoy things properly. I'm sorry if that's a bit graphic... am I giving you the right kind of information? Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: During these evening eating episodes, do you find yourself eating until you feel uncomfortably full?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I get *uncomfortably* full. I really try to be careful not to let it get to that point, because I know how bad that is for you. \n\nI might feel... you know, a little stuffed sometimes, like anyone would after a big meal, but it's not painful or anything like that. I usually stop before it gets to be too much. I try to listen to my body as best I can. \n\nI hope that's okay to say... I just don't think it reaches that level of being uncomfortable. Is that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating a lot even when you're not really hungry, especially during these evening episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. I guess that happens too. I really wish it didn't, because I know how it sounds, and I don't want you to think I'm just being gluttonous. But... yeah.\n\nA lot of the time, I'm actually not hungry at all. I mean, I might have just had a perfectly nice dinner with my wife, and I'll feel totally satisfied. But then... later on, I'll still find myself going back into the kitchen. I'll tell myself I'm just going to pick at a few leftovers, just a little taste, but... it almost always turns into this whole other full-on spread. \n\nI feel so foolish admitting that. I really do try to just stick to my regular meals, I promise. It's just hard sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not disappointing you with these answers... is this what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: After these evening eating episodes, do you find yourself feeling guilty, ashamed, or disgusted with yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, gosh. I really, really hate to admit this part. I want you to think well of me, and I try so hard to be good, but... yes. I do. \n\nAfterward... I just feel sick with myself. It’s hard because... well, I feel like everybody sees me as this organized, strong man who has everything under control. But then I’m just sitting there, feeling so incredibly ashamed and guilty. And honestly, just disgusted that I let it happen again. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds pathetic, and I really don't want you to think less of me for it. I'm trying to fix it, I promise. Did... did that answer your question okay? I hope I'm giving you what you need.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: During these evening eating episodes, do you find yourself eating until you feel uncomfortably full?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I get *uncomfortably* full. I really try to be careful not to let it get to that point, because I know how bad that is for you. \n\nI might feel... you know, a little stuffed sometimes, like anyone would after a big meal, but it's not painful or anything like that. I end up eating way past a normal plate. Like, if we have leftovers from an event or something—fried chicken, mac and cheese, sweet potatoes, rolls, things like that—I'll just start eating. And sometimes I don't stop until a whole tray or, gosh, half the fridge is just gone. I know it sounds bad, and I really don't mean to do it, but... is that the kind of thing you can help with? Does that make sense as a reason to be here?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed about this eating behavior, or has it started to affect your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't want to make too big a deal out of it, but... yes, it does bother me. I guess it has started to affect things a little bit. It's just hard to admit. \n\nUm, I usually do a lot of volunteer work—like helping out with community planning meetings and organizing local events—but lately, I've sort of pulled back from all of that. I just feel so drained, and... well, embarrassed, honestly. I'm just so afraid people will look at me and notice that I'm not holding things together the way I used to. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too silly or weak... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you feel like you can't stop or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yes. I guess so. It's a little hard to admit, because I really don't want you to think I just have zero willpower or anything like that. But... yeah. \n\nIt's almost like, once I take that very first bite, it just feels like the decision has already been made for me. I'll actually be standing there, telling myself in my head, 'George, stop now, you don't need any more,' but... it's like my hands just keep right on fixing another plate or opening another container anyway. I really do try to fight it, I promise, it just gets away from me. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it the right way... is that what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. I really try to be mindful about my meals, I promise I do, but... when it gets like that in the evenings, I guess I do speed up quite a bit. \n\nActually, if I'm being completely honest—and I really hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—I eat so fast during those times that I barely even taste the food. It's a bit sad, really, because I used to take so much pride in my cooking. But now... sometimes I'm just standing right there at the kitchen counter, swallowing these big bites before I’ve even finished chewing the last one. \n\nI know that probably sounds awful, and I really wish I could just slow down and enjoy things properly. I'm sorry if that's a bit graphic... am I giving you the right kind of information? Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: During these evening eating episodes, do you find yourself eating until you feel uncomfortably full?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I get *uncomfortably* full. I really try to be careful not to let it get to that point, because I know how bad that is for you. \n\nI might feel... you know, a little stuffed sometimes, like anyone would after a big meal, but it's not painful or anything like that. I usually stop before it gets to be too much. I try to listen to my body as best I can. \n\nI hope that's okay to say... I just don't think it reaches that level of being uncomfortable. Is that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating a lot even when you're not really hungry, especially during these evening episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. I guess that happens too. I really wish it didn't, because I know how it sounds, and I don't want you to think I'm just being gluttonous. But... yeah.\n\nA lot of the time, I'm actually not hungry at all. I mean, I might have just had a perfectly nice dinner with my wife, and I'll feel totally satisfied. But then... later on, I'll still find myself going back into the kitchen. I'll tell myself I'm just going to pick at a few leftovers, just a little taste, but... it almost always turns into this whole other full-on spread. \n\nI feel so foolish admitting that. I really do try to just stick to my regular meals, I promise. It's just hard sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not disappointing you with these answers... is this what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: After these evening eating episodes, do you find yourself feeling guilty, ashamed, or disgusted with yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, gosh. I really, really hate to admit this part. I want you to think well of me, and I try so hard to be good, but... yes. I do. \n\nAfterward... I just feel sick with myself. It’s hard because... well, I feel like everybody sees me as this organized, strong man who has everything under control. But then I’m just sitting there, feeling so incredibly ashamed and guilty. And honestly, just disgusted that I let it happen again. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds pathetic, and I really don't want you to think less of me for it. I'm trying to fix it, I promise. Did... did that answer your question okay? I hope I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes only happen at night, or do you also have similar experiences during other times of the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's mostly just at night. I really try to be good during the day. I mean, my wife is usually around, or I'm busy doing things around the house, so I keep to a pretty normal schedule. Breakfast, lunch... I try to eat like a normal person, you know? \n\nSometimes, maybe if I'm home alone in the afternoon, I might have a little extra, but it's never like what happens in the evenings. I wouldn't call it the same kind of episode at all. I really do my best to keep things under control during the day. I hope that's a good thing? I'm trying to do what I'm supposed to do... is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: How often do these evening eating episodes happen? Would you say they occur at least once a week, or is it less frequent than that?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's definitely more than once a week. I really, really wish I could say it was less, I do. I don't want you to think I'm completely out of control all the time. \n\nBut... for months now... gosh, I can't think of a single week where it didn't happen at least once. And if I'm being completely honest—which I'm trying to be, I promise—most weeks it's been several nights. Usually after my wife goes to bed and the house is quiet. \n\nI know that's probably a lot, and I'm so sorry. I really am trying to be better about it. Does... does that help you understand what's going on? I hope I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these evening eating episodes cause you a lot of distress or make it hard for you to function in your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, wow. That's... that's a really good question. I, um... I really try not to let it affect my day-to-day life. I want to be a good husband and a good member of the community, you know? I try so hard to keep up appearances and do what's expected of me.\n\nBut... well, I guess if I'm being completely truthful... and I know I need to be... it has been getting a little harder lately. I used to be really involved, you know? I'd help organize the neighborhood block parties, go to the community planning meetings... I was always the guy people could count on to get things done. \n\nLately, though... I've sort of... stepped back from all that. I just... I feel so drained the next day. I'm just so afraid they'll realize I'm not holding things together very well right now. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I shouldn't let it get in the way of my responsibilities like that. I really am trying to get back to being the guy I used to be. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or giving up. Is... is that what you meant by distress and functioning? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... oh, wow. That's... that's a little hard to talk about. I really don't want you to think I'm just... gorging myself, you know? I try to be mindful, I really do. 

But... well, I guess if I'm looking at it compared to what a normal person would eat... yeah, it's... it's a lot more. Like I said, it usually happens after my wife goes to bed. I'll go into the kitchen, and... well, we always have leftovers from different events or things my wife cooks. But then... I just keep going back. And before I know it... I've eaten way, way past what a normal person would have. Sometimes... sometimes I'll finish a whole tray of something, or it feels like half the fridge is just... gone.

I'm just so afraid people will look at me and notice that I'm not holding things together the way I used to. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too silly or weak... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you feel like you can't stop or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yes. I guess so. It's a little hard to admit, because I really don't want you to think I just have zero willpower or anything like that. But... yeah. \n\nIt's almost like, once I take that very first bite, it just feels like the decision has already been made for me. I'll actually be standing there, telling myself in my head, 'George, stop now, you don't need any more,' but... it's like my hands just keep right on fixing another plate or opening another container anyway. I really do try to fight it, I promise, it just gets away from me. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it the right way... is that what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. I really try to be mindful about my meals, I promise I do, but... when it gets like that in the evenings, I guess I do speed up quite a bit. \n\nActually, if I'm being completely honest—and I really hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—I eat so fast during those times that I barely even taste the food. It's a bit sad, really, because I used to take so much pride in my cooking. But now... sometimes I'm just standing right there at the kitchen counter, swallowing these big bites before I’ve even finished chewing the last one. \n\nI know that probably sounds awful, and I really wish I could just slow down and enjoy things properly. I'm sorry if that's a bit graphic... am I giving you the right kind of information? Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: During these evening eating episodes, do you find yourself eating until you feel uncomfortably full?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I get *uncomfortably* full. I really try to be careful not to let it get to that point, because I know how bad that is for you. \n\nI might feel... you know, a little stuffed sometimes, like anyone would after a big meal, but it's not painful or anything like that. I usually stop before it gets to be too much. I try to listen to my body as best I can. \n\nI hope that's okay to say... I just don't think it reaches that level of being uncomfortable. Is that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating a lot even when you're not really hungry, especially during these evening episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. I guess that happens too. I really wish it didn't, because I know how it sounds, and I don't want you to think I'm just being gluttonous. But... yeah.\n\nA lot of the time, I'm actually not hungry at all. I mean, I might have just had a perfectly nice dinner with my wife, and I'll feel totally satisfied. But then... later on, I'll still find myself going back into the kitchen. I'll tell myself I'm just going to pick at a few leftovers, just a little taste, but... it almost always turns into this whole other full-on spread. \n\nI feel so foolish admitting that. I really do try to just stick to my regular meals, I promise. It's just hard sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not disappointing you with these answers... is this what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: After these evening eating episodes, do you find yourself feeling guilty, ashamed, or disgusted with yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, gosh. I really, really hate to admit this part. I want you to think well of me, and I try so hard to be good, but... yes. I do. \n\nAfterward... I just feel sick with myself. It’s hard because... well, I feel like everybody sees me as this organized, strong man who has everything under control. But then I’m just sitting there, feeling so incredibly ashamed and guilty. And honestly, just disgusted that I let it happen again. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds pathetic, and I really don't want you to think less of me for it. I'm trying to fix it, I promise. Did... did that answer your question okay? I hope I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes only happen at night, or do you also have similar experiences during other times of the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's mostly just at night. I really try to be good during the day. I mean, my wife is usually around, or I'm busy doing things around the house, so I keep to a pretty normal schedule. Breakfast, lunch... I try to eat like a normal person, you know? \n\nSometimes, maybe if I'm home alone in the afternoon, I might have a little extra, but it's never like what happens in the evenings. I wouldn't call it the same kind of episode at all. I really do my best to keep things under control during the day. I hope that's a good thing? I'm trying to do what I'm supposed to do... is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: How often do these evening eating episodes happen? Would you say they occur at least once a week, or is it less frequent than that?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's definitely more than once a week. I really, really wish I could say it was less, I do. I don't want you to think I'm completely out of control all the time. \n\nBut... for months now... gosh, I can't think of a single week where it didn't happen at least once. And if I'm being completely honest—which I'm trying to be, I promise—most weeks it's been several nights. Usually after my wife goes to bed and the house is quiet. \n\nI know that's probably a lot, and I'm so sorry. I really am trying to be better about it. Does... does that help you understand what's going on? I hope I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these evening eating episodes cause you a lot of distress or make it hard for you to function in your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, wow. That's... that's a really good question. I, um... I really try not to let it affect my day-to-day life. I want to be a good husband and a good member of the community, you know? I try so hard to keep up appearances and do what's expected of me.\n\nBut... well, I guess if I'm being completely truthful... and I know I need to be... it has been getting a little harder lately. I used to be really involved, you know? I'd help organize the neighborhood block parties, go to the community planning meetings... I was always the guy people could count on to get things done. \n\nLately, though... I've sort of... stepped back from all that. I just... I feel so drained the next day. And, honestly, I get so embarrassed. I worry that if I'm around people, they're going to look at me and just... know. They'll see that I'm not the organized, put-together guy they thought I was. I'm just so afraid they'll realize I'm not holding things together very well right now. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I shouldn't let it get in the way of my responsibilities like that. I really am trying to get back to being the guy I used to be. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or giving up. Is... is that what you meant by distress and functioning? I hope I answered that the right way for you.\n\nDoctor: During these evening eating episodes, do you ever feel like you can’t stop eating, even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh. That's... that's a tough one to admit. I really, really try to have willpower, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting there not even trying to stop. \n\nBut... well... I guess if I'm being totally honest... once I take that very first bite, it... it kind of feels like the decision has already been made for me. It's almost like I'm on autopilot? Even while I'm sitting there, literally telling myself, 'George, you need to stop now, you're doing so well'... my hands just... they just keep fixing another plate or opening another container. \n\nIt's not that I don't want to stop, I really do! I just... I don't know why I can't in that moment. I'm so sorry, I know that sounds terrible and weak. I'm really working on it, though. Is... is that a common thing? I hope I'm not the only one who struggles with that part...\n\nDoctor: Would you say that during these evening episodes, you're eating a much larger amount of food in a short period of time compared to what most people would eat in similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, wow. I try to be mindful, I really do. \n\nBut... well, I guess if I'm looking at it compared to what a normal person would eat... yeah, it's... it's a lot more. Like I said, it usually happens after my wife goes to bed. I'll go into the kitchen, and... well, we always have leftovers from different events or things my wife cooks. And I'll start with just a plate... maybe some fried chicken, some mac and cheese, sweet potatoes, a roll or two. Just a normal plate, you know? \n\nBut then... I just keep going back. And before I know it... I've eaten way, way past what a normal person would have. Sometimes... sometimes I'll finish a whole tray of something, or it feels like half the fridge is just... gone. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful. I really am trying to be better about portion control. I don't want you to think I'm just completely gluttonous. Does... does that answer your question? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating in secret or hiding these eating episodes because you're embarrassed about them?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, gosh. Um... well, gosh, you know, I think... I think I might have mentioned this a little bit earlier, but... yes, it does kind of feel like that. I really, really don't want you to think I'm completely out of my mind or anything! I'm usually a very grounded person, I promise.

It's so strange, because I'm fully awake, you know? I'm sitting right there, and in my head, I'm literally saying, 'George, you really need to stop now, you're going to make yourself sick.' 

But it's almost like... like my hands aren't listening to my brain? They just keep reaching for the next container, or fixing another plate, even while I'm telling myself no. It's just hard to admit. \n\nUm, I usually do a lot of volunteer work—like helping out with community planning meetings and organizing local events—but lately, I've sort of pulled back from all of that. I just feel so drained, and... well, embarrassed, honestly. I'm just so afraid people will look at me and notice that I'm not holding things together the way I used to. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too silly or weak... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you feel like you can't stop or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yes. I guess so. It's a little hard to admit, because I really don't want you to think I just have zero willpower or anything like that. But... yeah. \n\nIt's almost like, once I take that very first bite, it just feels like the decision has already been made for me. I'll actually be standing there, telling myself in my head, 'George, stop now, you don't need any more,' but... it's like my hands just keep right on fixing another plate or opening another container anyway. I really do try to fight it, I promise, it just gets away from me. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it the right way... is that what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. I really try to be mindful about my meals, I promise I do, but... when it gets like that in the evenings, I guess I do speed up quite a bit. \n\nActually, if I'm being completely honest—and I really hope you won't judge me too harshly for saying this—I eat so fast during those times that I barely even taste the food. It's a bit sad, really, because I used to take so much pride in my cooking. But now... sometimes I'm just standing right there at the kitchen counter, swallowing these big bites before I’ve even finished chewing the last one. \n\nI know that probably sounds awful, and I really wish I could just slow down and enjoy things properly. I'm sorry if that's a bit graphic... am I giving you the right kind of information? Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: During these evening eating episodes, do you find yourself eating until you feel uncomfortably full?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I get *uncomfortably* full. I really try to be careful not to let it get to that point, because I know how bad that is for you. \n\nI might feel... you know, a little stuffed sometimes, like anyone would after a big meal, but it's not painful or anything like that. I usually stop before it gets to be too much. I try to listen to my body as best I can. \n\nI hope that's okay to say... I just don't think it reaches that level of being uncomfortable. Is that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating a lot even when you're not really hungry, especially during these evening episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. I guess that happens too. I really wish it didn't, because I know how it sounds, and I don't want you to think I'm just being gluttonous. But... yeah.\n\nA lot of the time, I'm actually not hungry at all. I mean, I might have just had a perfectly nice dinner with my wife, and I'll feel totally satisfied. But then... later on, I'll still find myself going back into the kitchen. I'll tell myself I'm just going to pick at a few leftovers, just a little taste, but... it almost always turns into this whole other full-on spread. \n\nI feel so foolish admitting that. I really do try to just stick to my regular meals, I promise. It's just hard sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not disappointing you with these answers... is this what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: After these evening eating episodes, do you find yourself feeling guilty, ashamed, or disgusted with yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, gosh. I really, really hate to admit this part. I want you to think well of me, and I try so hard to be good, but... yes. I do. \n\nAfterward... I just feel sick with myself. It’s hard because... well, I feel like everybody sees me as this organized, strong man who has everything under control. But then I’m just sitting there, feeling so incredibly ashamed and guilty. And honestly, just disgusted that I let it happen again. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds pathetic, and I really don't want you to think less of me for it. I'm trying to fix it, I promise. Did... did that answer your question okay? I hope I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes only happen at night, or do you also have similar experiences during other times of the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's mostly just at night. I really try to be good during the day. I mean, my wife is usually around, or I'm busy doing things around the house, so I keep to a pretty normal schedule. Breakfast, lunch... I try to eat like a normal person, you know? \n\nSometimes, maybe if I'm home alone in the afternoon, I might have a little extra, but it's never like what happens in the evenings. I wouldn't call it the same kind of episode at all. I really do my best to keep things under control during the day. I hope that's a good thing? I'm trying to do what I'm supposed to do... is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: How often do these evening eating episodes happen? Would you say they occur at least once a week, or is it less frequent than that?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's definitely more than once a week. I really, really wish I could say it was less, I do. I don't want you to think I'm completely out of control all the time. \n\nBut... for months now... gosh, I can't think of a single week where it didn't happen at least once. And if I'm being completely honest—which I'm trying to be, I promise—most weeks it's been several nights. Usually after my wife goes to bed and the house is quiet. \n\nI know that's probably a lot, and I'm so sorry. I really am trying to be better about it. Does... does that help you understand what's going on? I hope I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these evening eating episodes cause you a lot of distress or make it hard for you to function in your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, wow. That's... that's a really good question. I, um... I really try not to let it affect my day-to-day life. I want to be a good husband and a good member of the community, you know? I try so hard to keep up appearances and do what's expected of me.\n\nBut... well, I guess if I'm being completely truthful... and I know I need to be... it has been getting a little harder lately. I used to be really involved, you know? I'd help organize the neighborhood block parties, go to the community planning meetings... I was always the guy people could count on to get things done. \n\nLately, though... I've sort of... stepped back from all that. I just... I feel so drained the next day. And, honestly, I get so embarrassed. I worry that if I'm around people, they're going to look at me and just... know. They'll see that I'm not the organized, put-together guy they thought I was. I'm just so afraid they'll realize I'm not holding things together very well right now. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I shouldn't let it get in the way of my responsibilities like that. I really am trying to get back to being the guy I used to be. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or giving up. Is... is that what you meant by distress and functioning? I hope I answered that the right way for you.\n\nDoctor: During these evening eating episodes, do you ever feel like you can’t stop eating, even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh. That's... that's a tough one to admit. I really, really try to have willpower, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting there not even trying to stop. \n\nBut... well... I guess if I'm being totally honest... once I take that very first bite, it... it kind of feels like the decision has already been made for me. It's almost like I'm on autopilot? Even while I'm sitting there, literally telling myself, 'George, you need to stop now, you're doing so well'... my hands just... they just keep fixing another plate or opening another container. \n\nIt's not that I don't want to stop, I really do! I just... I don't know why I can't in that moment. I'm so sorry, I know that sounds terrible and weak. I'm really working on it, though. Is... is that a common thing? I hope I'm not the only one who struggles with that part...\n\nDoctor: Would you say that during these evening episodes, you're eating a much larger amount of food in a short period of time compared to what most people would eat in similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, wow. That's... that's a little hard to talk about. I really don't want you to think I'm just... gorging myself, you know? I try to be mindful, I really do. \n\nBut... well, I guess if I'm looking at it compared to what a normal person would eat... yeah, it's... it's a lot more. Like I said, it usually happens after my wife goes to bed. I'll go into the kitchen, and... well, we always have leftovers from different events or things my wife cooks. And I'll start with just a plate... maybe some fried chicken, some mac and cheese, sweet potatoes, a roll or two. Just a normal plate, you know? \n\nBut then... I just keep going back. And before I know it... I've eaten way, way past what a normal person would have. Sometimes... sometimes I'll finish a whole tray of something, or it feels like half the fridge is just... gone. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful. I really am trying to be better about portion control. I don't want you to think I'm just completely gluttonous. Does... does that answer your question? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating in secret or hiding these eating episodes because you're embarrassed about them?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, gosh. That's... that's a very direct question. I, um... I really try not to be deceitful, you know? I believe in being honest with my wife, we have a wonderful marriage and I don't want to keep things from her. I'm a good husband, I really try to be.\n\nBut... well... yes. I guess I do wait until she's asleep. It's not that I'm trying to be sneaky! It's just... I know she worries about my health, and she works so hard, I just don't want to upset her or make her think I'm not taking care of myself. If she saw how much I was eating, she'd be so disappointed, and I just... I can't bear to see that look on her face. \n\nSo, I wait until the house is quiet. And in the morning, I always make sure to take the trash out early, or I'll rearrange the containers in the fridge so it doesn't look like so much is missing. I just... I feel so ashamed of it. I'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't hide things. I really want to be better about being open. Is... is it normal to feel that embarrassed about it? I hope you don't think less of me for not being completely upfront with her...\n\nDoctor: During these evening eating episodes, do you feel like you're in a trance or that you're not fully in control of your actions, almost like you're watching yourself do it but can't stop?\n\nPatient: Um... well, gosh, you know, I think... I think I might have mentioned this a little bit earlier, but... yes, it does kind of feel like that. I really, really don't want you to think I'm completely out of my mind or anything! I'm usually a very grounded person, I promise. \n\nBut... yeah, like I was saying before, once I take that very first bite... it's like a switch flips. It really does feel like the decision to keep eating has just... already been made for me. It's so strange, because I'm fully awake, you know? I'm sitting right there, and in my head, I'm literally saying, 'George, you really need to stop now, you're going to make yourself sick.' \n\nBut it's almost like... like my hands aren't listening to my brain? They just keep reaching for the next container, or fixing another plate, even while I'm telling myself no. It's... it's really embarrassing to admit that I can't even control my own hands in that moment. I'm so sorry, I know I must sound so weak-willed when I describe it like that. I really am trying so hard to be more present and mindful. I hope I'm explaining it in a way that makes sense... does that sound like what you were asking about?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
